**Phase 4.4 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-28):**

- `releases.verify_version_code` is a strictly read-only, operation-scoped tool with permission exactly `read`, an empty model-facing input schema, no verifier, no approval callback, and expected Phase 2 result `VERIFICATION_SKIPPED`. `packageName`, the exact operator-selected `targetTrack`, and the verified Phase 4.3 `{ versionCode, sha256 }` identity are composition-bound; the model cannot supply them.
- Before comparison, Phase 4.2 session preflight loads the tracked session, checks package binding and local expiry, and confirms the same remote edit with `getEdit`. It then calls `bundles.list` on that exact edit and requires the exact uploaded `versionCode` + SHA-256 pair. A missing or mismatched pair fails as `UPLOADED_BUNDLE_NOT_FOUND`; no upload is retried or attempted.
- The target track is read exactly once through `edits.tracks.get` with the unchanged operator-bound name. A nonblank returned track identifier must match; mismatches fail as `TRACK_MISMATCH`. Missing/unreadable track state fails safely; no track is created or substituted.
- Comparison is strict: `uploadedVersionCode > currentMaxVersionCode`. All valid `versionCodes` from all releases are flattened, regardless of release status or array order, and the maximum is selected with lossless `BigInt` comparison. Equal and lower values fail closed as `VERSION_CODE_NOT_GREATER`; canonical decimal strings are emitted and BigInt is never serialized.
- A target track with zero releases or no versionCodes yields `currentMaxVersionCode: null` and `comparison: "no-current-version"`, which is a successful comparison state; zero is never invented as a remote baseline. This verifies the selected target track only and does not claim global app-wide version history safety.
- The tool has no path to `edits.insert`, `bundles.upload`, `tracks.update`/`patch`/`create`, `edits.validate`, `edits.commit`/`delete`, or `reviews.reply`. No release configuration, release notes, rollout change, publication, browser action, live Google request, or new dependency was added. B1 remains resolved only for earlier read/connectivity evidence; B3 remains OPEN for Node 24 verification.
- Phase 4.4 tests cover strict numeric comparisons, unsorted and duplicate releases/versionCodes, empty tracks, malformed data, very large lossless values, session preflight, exact bundle identity, exact track forwarding/mismatch, safe failures, serializer allowlisting, permission/approval/verification behavior, and real Phase 2 `ToolRegistry`/`runAgent` integration against fake Publisher boundaries.
- Final local evidence: **968/968 tests across 42 files**; build, lint, typecheck, and format check all exit 0 on Node v26.9.0; built fake-only smoke passes success (`101 > 100`), equal/lower blocking, empty-track success, unsorted maximum `100`, large-integer BigInt comparison, wrong-hash blocking before track read, zero mutation calls, zero approval events, `VERIFICATION_SKIPPED`, zero network, zero browser, and audit-safe serialization.

**Phase 4.5 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-29):**

- `releases.inspect_target_track` is a strictly read-only, operation-scoped tool with permission exactly `read`, an empty model-facing input schema, no verifier, no approval callback, and expected Phase 2 result `VERIFICATION_SKIPPED`. `packageName` and the exact operator-selected `targetTrack` are composition-bound; the model cannot supply them.

**Phase 4.6 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-29):**

- `releases.configure_release` is a `write` tool because `edits.tracks.update` mutates the active draft edit, but it does not publish the app and does not invalidate another edit. Under current Phase 2 policy, `write` is allowed without human approval; a real post-action verifier is mandatory. Tests and smoke assert approval events `0` and verifier status `VERIFIED` on success.
- The tool is composition-bound and operation-scoped. The model-facing authoritative input is exactly `{}` and rejects every key. `packageName`, `targetTrack`, `releaseName`, `releaseStatus`, `initialRolloutFraction`, the verified Phase 4.3 `{ versionCode, sha256 }` identity, optional `retainVersionCodes`, edit-session path, credentials, commit intent, and retry settings are not model-selected.
- The installed `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations and generated implementation were inspected. `edits.tracks.update` accepts `packageName`, `editId`, `track`, and `requestBody: Schema$Track`, returns `Schema$Track`, and generates `PUT /androidpublisher/v3/applications/{packageName}/edits/{editId}/tracks/{track}`. The installed `Schema$Track` declaration states that `releases[]` means active releases in a read and desired changes in an update. `Schema$TrackRelease` documents `name`, `versionCodes`, `releaseNotes`, `status`, `userFraction`, country targeting, and update priority; `userFraction` is constrained to `0 < fraction < 1` for `inProgress`/`halted`; version codes include codes intentionally retained from previous releases.
- Current official Android Publisher documentation was consulted for `edits.tracks.update` and the Track/TrackRelease resource. The Phase 4.6 request intentionally supports only `draft`, `inProgress`, and `completed`; `statusUnspecified` and `halted` are rejected, with `halted` reserved for Phase 4.13. `edits.tracks.update` affects only the active edit; nothing becomes live until a later `edits.commit`.
- Release intent validation is strict and local: release name must be a nonblank string and is trimmed without imposing an undocumented length limit; `draft` and `completed` require the fraction field to be absent; `inProgress` requires a finite fraction strictly between `0` and `1`; supplied invalid values are rejected rather than repaired or silently removed.
- Before mutation the tool loads the tracked Phase 4.2 session, checks package binding and expiry, confirms the exact remote edit with `getEdit`, confirms the exact Phase 4.3 bundle identity with `bundles.list`, reads the exact unchanged operator-bound target with `tracks.get`, confirms returned track identity, and reruns the strict Phase 4.4 lossless version rule against fresh state. A fresh `uploadedVersionCode <= currentMaxVersionCode` fails as `VERSION_CODE_NOT_GREATER` with `tracks.update` count `0`.
- A fresh target track with any `draft`, `inProgress`, or `halted` release fails closed as `OUTSTANDING_RELEASE_EXISTS`; PlayOps does not auto-halt, auto-complete, overwrite, or replace it. A track containing only completed releases may proceed. An `inProgress` first release with no current target-track version fails as `STAGED_ROLLOUT_REQUIRES_EXISTING_RELEASE`; PlayOps never falls back to `completed`.
- The default new-release version-code set is exactly the uploaded Phase 4.3 versionCode. Optional `retainVersionCodes` are caller-bound only, canonicalized losslessly, deduplicated, sorted numerically with `BigInt`, and accepted only when every retained code exists on the fresh target track. Historic codes are never copied automatically. The uploaded code is always included.
- The update body is PlayOps-owned and narrow: `{ track: targetTrack, releases: [{ name, versionCodes, status, userFraction? }] }`. `userFraction` is omitted entirely for `draft`/`completed`; `releaseNotes`, `countryTargeting`, and `inAppUpdatePriority` are never supplied or copied from older releases. Existing release objects are not reconstructed because update `releases[]` represents desired changes.
- The Publisher boundary adds only `updateTrack`, calls `edits.tracks.update` with exact package/edit/track/body and `{ retry: false }`, validates the returned track identity and structural shape, and maps safe failures. No Phase 1.5 read-retry wrapper is used around this mutation. 429, 500, timeout, `ECONNRESET`, and ambiguous transport failures each result in exactly one update attempt and an uncertain mutation outcome; no automatic retry or rollback exists.
- The mandatory verifier performs a fresh `tracks.get` on the exact same target track after update. It identifies the intended release by the uploaded versionCode, then confirms exact target identity, release name, status, canonical versionCode set, uploaded/retained membership, and fraction semantics. Version-code arrays are compared as canonical lossless numeric sets, not by array order. A read-back mismatch returns `VERIFICATION_FAILED`, sets `externalStateUncertain: true`, performs no second update, and never commits or rolls back.
- A small additive runtime safety signal was required for the explicit Phase 4.6 acceptance that known pre-mutation guards remain `externalStateUncertain: false`: `ReleaseError.externalStateUncertain: false` is honored only when a domain error explicitly supplies it; all existing mutation failures without that explicit opt-in retain the prior uncertain behavior. This does not alter approval, verification, serialization, batching, retry, or lifecycle rules for existing tools; the Phase 4.6 stale-version integration proves `VERSION_CODE_NOT_GREATER`, update `0`, and uncertainty `false`.
- The GET → UPDATE → GET sequence is not an atomic compare-and-set transaction. Google does not provide PlayOps a lock for this sequence. Fresh session/bundle/track/version checks, exact identity, and read-back reduce risk but do not eliminate a concurrent Play Console/API race.
- Phase 4.6 has no path to `edits.insert`, `bundles.upload`, `edits.validate`, `edits.commit`, `edits.delete`, `tracks.patch`, `tracks.create`, or `reviews.reply`. Phase 4.7 owns release notes; Phase 4.8 owns formal edit validation; Phase 4.9/4.10 own approval and commit; Phase 4.12/4.13 own later rollout control and halt/resume.
- Focused tests: **72/72 across 3 files** (`releases-configure-release.test.ts`, `releases-configure-release-integration.test.ts`, `publisher-track-update.test.ts`). Full suite after Phase 4.6: **1107/1107 tests across 47 files**. Final gates on local Node v26.9.0: build, lint, typecheck, and format check all exit 0. Built fake-only smoke `playops-phase46-built-smoke.mjs` passes draft/completed/inProgress success, invalid status/fraction, outstanding-release guards, first-release staged guard, fresh stale-version guard, one-attempt mutation failures, exact read-back verification, mismatch uncertainty, omitted later-phase fields, zero approval, zero forbidden mutations, zero network, zero browser, and audit safety.
- Runtime dependencies remain exactly 4 and `@googleapis/androidpublisher` remains `42.1.0`; `package.json` and `package-lock.json` hashes remain `d1ca499f3408e507e44d4ad7a88107f8da5ed786f8b58be38132f17718008a53` and `8f8b2805eb7acdcea9dd67212071e5df84c24ab139e741d50b7348d58599017e`. Review Agent source and all unrelated Phase 4 behavior remain unchanged; no live Google call, credential read, real AAB, browser action, or new dependency was used. Live `edits.tracks.update` authorization remains **UNVERIFIED**. B1 remains resolved only for prior read/connectivity evidence; B3 remains OPEN because Node 24 was not run.

**Phase 4.7 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-29):**

- `releases.attach_release_notes` is a `write` tool: it mutates only the active edit and does not publish or invalidate another edit. Under current Phase 2 semantics it requires no human approval but has a real post-action verifier. No approval event is emitted.
- Notes, target track, Phase 4.6 configured-release identity, uploaded bundle identity, package, session path, and retry policy are composition-bound. The authoritative model input is exactly `{}`; the model cannot supply locale, text, target track, package, versionCode, release identity, or commit intent.
- Installed `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations and official Android Publisher documentation were consulted for `Schema$LocalizedText`, `Schema$TrackRelease`, `Schema$Track`, and `edits.tracks.update`. PlayOps uses the structured `{ language, text }` representation, not Play Console wrapper text. Locale syntax is validated with built-in `Intl.getCanonicalLocales`; this proves BCP-47 syntax/canonicalization only, not that Google Play supports the locale for a particular app.
- Local note validation requires at least one note, rejects blank/whitespace locales and text, rejects malformed BCP-47 tags, rejects duplicate locales after canonicalization (`en-us` and `en-US` collide), rejects NUL/unsafe controls while allowing normal tab/newline/carriage-return formatting, and enforces **500 Unicode code points** with `Array.from(text).length`. Text is never truncated, translated, summarized, spell-corrected, or otherwise rewritten. Canonical locale tags are sorted deterministically; note content remains exact operator text. Promotional-content guidance is documented as operator guidance only; no heuristic detector was added.
- Fresh preflight reuses Phase 4.6 safety: managed session load/package/expiry, exact remote `getEdit`, exact Phase 4.3 bundle identity through `bundles.list`, and exact target-track `tracks.get` with identity confirmation. The fresh track must contain exactly one release containing the uploaded versionCode. Zero matches fail as `CONFIGURED_RELEASE_NOT_FOUND`; multiple matches fail as `CONFIGURED_RELEASE_AMBIGUOUS`.
- The matched release must still equal the bound Phase 4.6 intent: name, status, canonical versionCode set, and staged-fraction semantics. Any change fails before mutation as `CONFIGURED_RELEASE_CHANGED` with `externalStateUncertain: false`; notes are never attached to a stale or unexpected release.
- The update body round-trips every normalized active release in fresh order. For every release it preserves name, versionCodes, status, userFraction, existing releaseNotes, countryTargeting, and inAppUpdatePriority. Only the matching release's `releaseNotes` differs, becoming the validated canonical note set. Existing normalized state that cannot be safely round-tripped fails before mutation as `TRACK_STATE_NOT_ROUNDTRIPPABLE`; no lossy partial update is sent.
- The target release is identified by uploaded versionCode, not name alone. Release ordering is preserved. Note ordering is deterministic by canonical locale, while equality and verification compare notes as exact locale→text maps, independent of array order.
- If the fresh target release already has exactly the intended canonical note set, the tool performs no mutation and returns `updated: false`; permission remains `write` because the capability may mutate. The real verifier still reads `tracks.get` and confirms the snapshot.
- A real post-action verifier reads the exact target track and compares the complete expected normalized snapshot: target identity, release count/order, every unrelated release, target name/status/versionCodes/fraction/country targeting/update priority, and exact localized note maps. It does not merely check that notes exist. A mismatch returns `VERIFICATION_FAILED`, marks external state uncertain, and triggers no retry, rollback, validation, or commit.
- The mutation wrapper uses `edits.tracks.update` exactly once with `retry:false`; no Phase 1.5 read retry wraps it. 429, 500, 502, 503, 504, timeout, `ECONNRESET`, and ambiguous transport outcomes are never retried. Pre-mutation domain failures use the Phase 4.6 explicit `externalStateUncertain: false` signal; mutation/verification failures remain uncertain.
- The GET → UPDATE → GET path is not an atomic compare-and-set transaction. Fresh identity checks, full-state desired update construction, and full snapshot read-back mitigate but cannot eliminate a concurrent Play Console/API race.
- Phase 4.7 has no path to `edits.insert`, `bundles.upload`, `edits.validate`, `edits.commit`, `edits.delete`, `tracks.patch`, `tracks.create`, or `reviews.reply`. It does not change release name, versionCodes, status, rollout, country targeting, update priority, or halt/resume state. Phase 4.8 owns formal edit validation; Phase 4.9/4.10 own approval and commit.

**Phase 4.8 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-29):**

- `releases.validate_edit` is permission exactly `read`, despite the Android Publisher HTTP method being POST. The classification follows semantic effect: `edits.validate` checks whether the active edit can be committed at that moment but does not apply edit changes to the live app and does not change the edit's release/track state. It therefore has no approval callback, no human approval path, no mutation verifier, and the Phase 2 runtime records `VERIFICATION_SKIPPED`.
- The authoritative model input is exactly `{}`. Package name, managed session path, edit id, credentials, retry configuration, track, release status, versionCode, and commit intent are composition/session-bound. No nested LLM or 9Router call exists.
- The installed `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations and generated surface were inspected: `Params$Resource$Edits$Validate` contains only `packageName` and `editId`; `Resource$Edits.validate` accepts those path parameters with no request body and returns `Schema$AppEdit`; `Schema$AppEdit` contains output-only `id` and `expiryTimeSeconds`. Official Android Publisher documentation was consulted for the same `POST /androidpublisher/v3/applications/{packageName}/edits/{editId}:validate` contract and commit-readiness semantics.
- Fresh preflight loads the existing `ReleaseEditSession`, enforces package binding and local non-expiry, calls `getEdit` for the exact tracked edit, requires the remote id to equal the tracked id, requires the remote expiry to be valid and equal to the tracked expiry, and rejects a remotely expired edit. It never opens, clears, replaces, uploads, updates tracks, deletes, or commits an edit. No session write occurs after success or failure.
- The narrow Publisher wrapper adds only `validateEdit`. It forwards exactly `{ packageName, editId }`, supplies no request body content, disables generated/Gaxios retry with `{ retry:false }`, and uses the existing bounded PlayOps `read` retry policy for transient 429/5xx/transport failures. No second retry implementation or mutation retry is introduced.
- Validation response data is untrusted. PlayOps requires a nonblank exact matching AppEdit id, a decimal-string `expiryTimeSeconds`, and a non-expired returned expiry using the existing lossless epoch comparison. A mismatched id returns `VALIDATION_RESPONSE_MISMATCH`; malformed response data returns `VALIDATION_RESPONSE_INVALID`; an expired validation result returns `EDIT_VALIDATION_EXPIRED`. The normalized domain result is only `{ valid: true, expiryTimeSeconds }`; raw AppEdit fields and transport metadata do not escape.
- Explicit Google validation rejection is surfaced as `EDIT_VALIDATION_FAILED` with a fixed safe message and the upstream cause retained only programmatically. The agent run fails closed and cannot continue as `COMPLETED`; because no PlayOps mutation was attempted, all validation/preflight failures retain `externalStateUncertain: false`.
- A successful validation means Google accepted the edit as committable **at that validation time only**. It is not human approval, not a durable commit authorization, and not a guarantee that a later Phase 4.9/4.10 commit will succeed; the edit may expire, be invalidated, or change before commit. Phase 4.9/4.10 must perform their own fresh safety/approval checks.
- Phase 4.8 has no path to `edits.insert`, `bundles.upload`, `tracks.update`, `tracks.patch`, `tracks.create`, `edits.commit`, `edits.delete`, or `reviews.reply`. The only Google operations in the validation path are exact `getEdit` preflight and `edits.validate`; no track/bundle/app mutation is performed.

Full suite after Phase 4.8: **1197/1197 tests across 52 files**. Final code gates on Node v26.9.0: build, lint, typecheck, and format check pass. Built smoke `playops-phase48-built-smoke.mjs` passes valid managed edit, exact `valid:true` output, read permission, `VERIFICATION_SKIPPED`, zero approval, bounded read retry, generated retry disabled, malformed/mismatched/expired/invalidated/rejected validation guards, `externalStateUncertain:false`, zero insert/upload/track update/commit/delete calls, safe audit/output, zero network, and zero browser.
- Runtime dependencies remain exactly 4; `@googleapis/androidpublisher` remains `42.1.0`; package/lock hashes remain `d1ca499f3408e507e44d4ad7a88107f8da5ed786f8b58be38132f17718008a53` / `8f8b2805eb7acdcea9dd67212071e5df84c24ab139e741d50b7348d58599017e`; Review Agent and Phase 2 runtime source remain unchanged; no live Google call, credential read, real AAB, browser action, or dependency addition occurred. Live `edits.validate` authorization remains **UNVERIFIED**. B1 remains resolved only for prior read/connectivity evidence; B3 remains OPEN because Node 24 was not run.

**Phase 4.9 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-29):**

- The official commit contract was checked against `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations and generated implementation. `edits.commit` is `POST /androidpublisher/v3/applications/{packageName}/edits/{editId}:commit`; `packageName` and `editId` are required; the request body is empty; success returns `AppEdit`; query parameters include `changesInReviewBehavior` and `changesNotSentForReview`. Google documents that committing applies the edit's changes to the application, unlike the prior draft-edit operations.
- Google’s current omitted/default review behavior is `CANCEL_IN_REVIEW_AND_SUBMIT`, which can cancel changes already in review and submit all changes for publishing. Because the installed SDK supports the field, PlayOps binds `ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW` as its fail-closed policy; an in-review conflict must fail instead of silently cancelling review. The Phase 4.9 builder rejects the risky alternate policy rather than falling back to Google’s default.
- `changesNotSentForReview` is workflow-bound, defaults explicitly to `false`, is included in the exact intent/request digest, and is not model-controlled. No Phase 4.9 CLI/UI option exposes it.
- `src/releases/commit-approval.ts` adds no Google adapter and no production `ToolDefinition`. It exports `createReleaseCommitIntent`, `createReleaseCommitStateDigest`, `createReleaseCommitRequestDigest`, `createReleaseCommitApprovalBinding`, and the reserved future tool name `releases.commit_edit`. Normal release composition explicitly does not register that tool; Phase 4.10 must supply the real executor before production exposure.
- `ReleaseCommitIntent` is immutable and workflow-bound. It includes version `1`, package, exact managed `editId`, target track, uploaded/configured `versionCode`, exact configured release name/status, rollout fraction or explicit absence, canonical note-language set, state digest, `ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW`, `changesNotSentForReview`, validation evidence expiry, and request digest. The builder requires a trusted successful Phase 4.8 validation result (`valid:true` plus decimal `expiryTimeSeconds`) but does not call `edits.validate`; validation is evidence, not approval, and Phase 4.10 must validate freshly before commit.
- The state digest is deterministic SHA-256 over the normalized target track: target track identity; every active release; canonical numeric version-code set; release name/status/fraction; exact localized note language/text pairs; country targeting; and in-app update priority. Release and note ordering is normalized according to domain set/map semantics, while exact note text remains inside the digest only. Credentials, tokens, session paths, raw Google objects, and transport metadata are excluded.
- The request digest is deterministic SHA-256 over the canonical exact request identity: tool name, package, edit id, target track, versionCode, release name/status, rollout fraction, note-language set, state digest, review behavior, `changesNotSentForReview`, and trusted validation evidence expiry. Object insertion order and summary formatting do not affect identity; any material package/edit/track/version/status/fraction/note/state/review-policy change does.
- The reusable approval binding has permission exactly `publish`, uses the existing Phase 2 `createRequestDigest`/`createSafeSummary` contract, and closes over the immutable intent. The safe human summary identifies the app, track, version, release, status, staged fraction, note languages, publish/apply effect, `ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW` policy, and fail-closed review behavior; it omits note text, credentials, tokens, raw edit id, digests, and session paths. The cryptographic request digest—not punctuation or summary prose—is authoritative.
- The real Phase 2 runtime was exercised with a test-only `releases.commit_edit` sentinel callback. No approval, denial, expired approval, mismatched digest, wrong/stale edit approval, or changed-intent approval reaches the callback; only one exact approval reaches it once. Existing approval TTL, ledger, audit, and replay semantics were reused unchanged; no global approval-engine redesign or bypass was introduced. Denial produces audit evidence but no usable approval capability.
- Phase 4.9 performs no `edits.commit`, `edits.insert`, bundle upload, track update/patch/create, edit deletion, or review reply. It performs no live Google, network, browser, credential, or real artifact operation. It does not claim live commit authorization. B1 remains resolved only for prior read/connectivity evidence; B3 remains OPEN because Node 24 was not run.

**Phase 4.10 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-29):**

- The installed `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations and generated implementation were inspected for `edits.commit`, `Params$Resource$Edits$Commit`, and `Schema$AppEdit`. The generated method is `POST /androidpublisher/v3/applications/{packageName}/edits/{editId}:commit`; only `packageName` and `editId` are path parameters; `changesInReviewBehavior` and `changesNotSentForReview` are query parameters; no request body is sent; success returns `AppEdit` with output-only `id` and `expiryTimeSeconds`.
- Official Google semantics were recorded: commit applies changes from the edit back to the application, so the operation crosses the publish boundary and changes are no longer merely draft/edit state. Committing can invalidate other active edits for the application, propagation can take time, and successful commit response alone does not satisfy Phase 4.11 live-release read-back.
- The production tool is `releases.commit_edit` with permission exactly `publish` and authoritative model input exactly `{}`. Operation-scoped production composition registers it only when supplied a trusted Phase 4.9 `ReleaseCommitIntent`; no model or CLI field can select package, edit, track, release, notes, state digest, retry, or commit policy.
- The tool reuses `createReleaseCommitApprovalBinding` without redefining digest or approval semantics. The human summary explicitly warns that committing may invalidate other active edits, including uncommitted work owned by other users; summary wording remains non-authoritative and does not change the cryptographic request digest.
- After Phase 2 approval and immediately before mutation, the tool loads the local `ReleaseEditSession`, requires exact package/edit identity and validation-evidence expiry binding, checks local expiry, confirms exact remote `getEdit`, reads the exact composition-bound target track, recomputes the same Phase 4.9 normalized SHA-256 state digest, and blocks as `COMMIT_STATE_CHANGED` when any approved release/track state changes. It never rebuilds or silently re-approves intent.
- A fresh `edits.validate` call is mandatory after the state digest matches and immediately before commit. Successful fresh validation is not approval, does not extend approval TTL, and does not change the request digest. Mismatched, malformed, expired, rejected, or otherwise failed validation blocks with `externalStateUncertain:false`; commit attempt remains zero.
- The only commit policy accepted and transmitted is `changesInReviewBehavior=ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW` and `changesNotSentForReview=false`. `CANCEL_IN_REVIEW_AND_SUBMIT`, `CHANGES_IN_REVIEW_BEHAVIOR_TYPE_UNSPECIFIED`, omission of the fail-closed review policy, and `true` for `changesNotSentForReview` are not allowed. Google’s omitted/default behavior remains documented as the risky `CANCEL_IN_REVIEW_AND_SUBMIT` behavior.
- The narrow Publisher `commitEdit` wrapper is the only new Google mutation adapter. It forwards exact package/edit/policy parameters, no body, and `{ retry:false }`; it does not use `executeWithRetry`, Gaxios retry, or any recovery attempt. 429/5xx/timeout/`ECONNRESET` and other ambiguous transport outcomes make exactly one attempt and remain uncertain.
- Explicit structured in-review rejection is recognized only from allowlisted structured metadata, never raw human error text, and maps to `CHANGES_ALREADY_IN_REVIEW` with `externalStateUncertain:false`; the session remains tracked and no retry occurs. Other explicit client rejection responses map to safe `COMMIT_REJECTED`; malformed/mismatched successful responses map to `COMMIT_RESPONSE_INVALID` with `externalStateUncertain:true` because Google may already have accepted the commit.
- A confirmed commit response must contain the exact committed edit id and a valid, non-expired decimal `expiryTimeSeconds`. Only after response validation does PlayOps call `ReleaseEditSessionStore.clear()` exactly once and read the store back, requiring no active session. Clear/read-back failure maps to `COMMIT_SESSION_CLEANUP_FAILED`, never retries commit, and remains uncertain because external publication may already have happened.
- The narrow Phase 4.10 verifier checks only exact commit-result identity and local managed-session closure. It does not call a post-commit Google release read endpoint and does not claim propagation. Safe output is `{ committed:true, targetTrack, versionCode, releaseStatus, changesInReviewBehavior:"ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW", liveReleaseVerified:false }`; raw AppEdit, approval token, credentials, session path, note text, and raw errors are excluded.
- Commit success/failure is recorded through the existing append-only audit ledger with safe request digest, publish permission, attempt/result/error code, uncertainty, and `liveReleaseVerified:false` where applicable. Audit-write failure after confirmed commit becomes `COMMIT_AUDIT_FAILED` and remains uncertain. No automatic `edits.insert` or new edit follows commit.

Coverage includes exact SDK request/policy/body/retry behavior; production registration; empty model input; exact Phase 4.9 binding; no/denied/expired/mismatched/wrong-tool approval; stale edit/state; target-track mismatch; status/fraction/note/version/unrelated-state changes; fresh validation success/rejection/mismatch/malformed/expiry; review conflict; ambiguous commit; malformed response; one-attempt guarantee; session cleanup/read-back; cleanup/audit failure; safe result; and no Phase 4.11 read-back.

**Phase 4.11 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-30):**

- The installed `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations and generated implementation were inspected for `applications.tracks.releases.list`, `Schema$ListReleaseSummariesResponse`, `Schema$ReleaseSummary`, `Schema$ArtifactSummary`, the release lifecycle field, `edits.insert`, `edits.tracks.get`, and `edits.delete`. The direct method is `GET /androidpublisher/v3/{+parent}/releases` with `parent=applications/{packageName}/tracks/{track}`; the installed response exposes `releases[]` and no `nextPageToken`, with Google documenting a maximum of 20 release summaries. `edits.delete` is a separate no-body `DELETE` operation. Official Google documentation was consulted for the direct release-summary endpoint, lifecycle limitations, edit insertion/deletion, and active-edit concurrency semantics.
- Layer A is `releases.inspect_committed_release`, permission exactly `read`, authoritative model input exactly `{}`, no approval, no edit creation, and no mutation. Package, custom/form-factor track, expected versionCode, expected release name, and trusted committed intent are composition-bound. The Publisher adapter constructs the exact parent; generated retry is disabled and the existing bounded PlayOps read retry is used. Release summaries are projected to PlayOps-owned `{releaseName, track, versionCodes, releaseLifecycleState}`; generated/raw Google types do not escape. The installed response has no pagination token, so one complete response is consumed rather than assuming a first page.
- Layer A identifies the expected release by active-artifact versionCode and requires exactly one match, exact track identity, and expected release name. Version codes are converted only after safe-integer validation and remain canonical decimal strings. Zero matches return `COMMITTED_RELEASE_NOT_OBSERVED`; duplicates return `COMMITTED_RELEASE_AMBIGUOUS`. `RELEASE_LIFECYCLE_STATE_PUBLISHED` is retained exactly as coarse lifecycle evidence; it is not mapped to `TrackRelease.status` and does not prove `completed`, `inProgress`, `halted`, exact rollout fraction, or release notes. Layer A returns `exactTrackStateVerified:false` and never claims full `liveReleaseVerified`.
- Layer B is `releases.verify_committed_release`, permission exactly `destructive`, with a separate approval binding; it never reuses the Phase 4.9 publish approval. The human summary warns that a temporary Google Play edit may invalidate another active edit owned by the same API user, states that no release mutation or commit is performed, and states that the temporary edit is deleted after inspection. The digest binds package, target track, versionCode, expected release name, exact status, rollout fraction or absence, operation kind, and exact trusted release-note content without putting note text in the summary/audit.
- Layer B always performs a fresh Layer A direct read first. If the expected release is absent or ambiguous, it returns before `edits.insert`; no temporary edit, exact track read, or delete is attempted, and the operator may retry later. Before any insert, the local `ReleaseEditSessionStore` must be empty; approval never overrides a locally managed edit guard. An empty local store cannot prove another remote process has no active edit because Google exposes no safe active-edit listing operation; this remote limitation is why human destructive approval remains mandatory.
- After the direct summary succeeds and the local store is empty, Layer B makes exactly one `edits.insert` attempt with generated retry disabled, validates the exact temporary edit id and non-expired `expiryTimeSeconds`, does not save it in the normal managed session store, reads the exact target through `edits.tracks.get` with bounded read retry, and compares exactly one matching `TrackRelease` by versionCode. It verifies release name, exact status, fraction presence/value semantics, and exact trusted localized notes. It never derives exact status or fraction from lifecycle state.
- A trustworthy temporary edit is deleted exactly once after successful verification or deterministic state/read failure, using only that exact temporary id. Delete is non-retried. A cleanup failure returns `VERIFICATION_EDIT_CLEANUP_FAILED` with `externalStateUncertain:true`; an ambiguous insert with no trustworthy id is not retried and is never guessed for deletion. No path calls `edits.commit`, bundle upload, track update/patch/create, or review reply.
- The Layer B verifier performs no Google operation and no second temporary edit. It validates captured normalized Layer A/Layer B evidence, the expected trusted-state digest, exact output identity/status/fraction, cleanup success, `liveReleaseVerified:true`, and `servingPropagationVerified:false`. Full success means the direct release-summary endpoint observed the release, a fresh current-state edit snapshot exposed the exact configured `TrackRelease`, and temporary cleanup succeeded. It does not claim device, CDN, or serving propagation.
- Focused tests: **33/33 across 4 files** (`publisher-release-summaries.test.ts`, `publisher-edit-delete.test.ts`, `releases-readback.test.ts`, `releases-readback-integration.test.ts`). Coverage includes direct read permission/no approval, exact parent and custom track, release identity/zero/duplicate/pagination-contract/lossless version handling, coarse `PUBLISHED` lifecycle semantics, short-circuit before insert, separate approval denial/expiry/mismatch, local session guard, exact draft/completed/in-progress state and fraction checks, note mismatch, one-attempt insert/delete, cleanup failure, ambiguous insert, safe audit, and verifier no-second-edit behavior. Full regression `NODE_ENV=development npm run test:run -- --reporter=dot` → **1305/1305 across 60 files**. Final gates on local Node v26.9.0: build, lint, typecheck, and format check all pass. Built fake-only smoke `node /home/dhikrama/.hermes/cache/scratch/playops-phase411-readback-smoke.mjs` → `BUILT_PHASE411_READBACK_SMOKE PASS { scenarios:21, directReadPermission:"read", deepPermission:"destructive", summaryCalls:1, insertCalls:1, trackGetCalls:1, deleteCalls:1, commitCalls:0, uploadCalls:0, trackUpdateCalls:0, networkCalls:0, browserCalls:0 }`.
- Scope/dependency/Git review: runtime dependencies remain exactly 4; `package.json` hash is `d1ca499f3408e507e44d4ad7a88107f8da5ed786f8b58be38132f17718008a53`; `package-lock.json` hash is `8f8b2805eb7acdcea9dd67212071e5df84c24ab139e741d50b7348d58599017e`; no live Google call, credential read, browser action, real AAB, or dependency addition occurred. Phase 4.1–4.10 and Phase 2 runtime behavior remain regression-verified. B1 remains resolved only for earlier read/connectivity evidence; live direct-summary, `edits.insert`, and `edits.delete` authorization remain **UNVERIFIED**. B3 remains OPEN because Node 24 was not run. Repository remains unborn `master` with no valid `HEAD`; no repository commit was created; `git diff --check` and `git diff --cached --check` pass.

**Phase 4.12 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-30):**

- The installed `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations/generated client and current official Android Publisher documentation were inspected for `edits.insert`, `edits.tracks.get`, `edits.tracks.update`, `edits.validate`, `edits.commit`, `edits.delete`, `applications.tracks.releases.list`, `Schema$TrackRelease`, `Track.releases`, `TrackRelease.status`, and `TrackRelease.userFraction`. The documented staged-rollout contract is preserved: `userFraction` is strictly between 0 and 1 and is valid only for `inProgress` or `halted`; Phase 4.12 supports only advancement of an already-`inProgress` release.
- `src/releases/rollout-approval.ts` adds the separate exact `increase_staged_rollout` intent and approval binding. It binds package, exact target track, versionCode-primary release identity, release name, expected current `inProgress` status, expected current fraction, requested new fraction, normalized current-track state digest, `ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW`, `changesNotSentForReview:false`, and an exact request digest. It is not a reuse of Phase 4.9 publish approval or Phase 4.11 verification approval.
- The operation is `releases.update_rollout_fraction` with permission exactly `destructive`; model-facing authoritative input is exactly `{}`. Package, track, versionCode, release name, current state, and new fraction are workflow/composition-bound. The safe summary states current/new rollout percentages, that status remains `inProgress`, that live user eligibility changes, that Google Play will receive a commit, that edit creation may invalidate another active edit, that `ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW` is used, and that commit is not automatically retried. It omits notes, edit ids, tokens, credentials, and raw state.
- The policy is monotonic and exact: `0 < currentFraction < newFraction < 1`. No-op, decrease, zero, negative, one, greater-than-one, NaN, infinity, missing fraction, halted, draft, completed, and status-unspecified states fail closed. No fraction is rounded or compared as a formatted percentage. Phase 4.12 never halts, resumes, completes, creates a release, or changes any status; Phase 4.13 remains untouched.
- Before the destructive edit, the workflow reuses Phase 4.11 Layer A direct release-summary observation and requires exactly one expected active-artifact versionCode/release identity. A missing or ambiguous direct observation stops before `edits.insert`. The local `ReleaseEditSessionStore` is then rechecked; any active managed edit returns `MANAGED_EDIT_ALREADY_OPEN` with operational insert zero, and approval never overrides the guard.
- The operational lifecycle is one `edits.insert` attempt with generated retry disabled, exact id/expiry validation, and durable managed-session persistence; fresh exact `edits.get`/`edits.tracks.get`; exact versionCode-primary selection; `inProgress` and approved-current-fraction checks; one `edits.tracks.update`; same-edit pre-commit `tracks.get`; one `edits.validate`; and one `edits.commit`. Mutation calls use `{ retry:false }`; no mutation retry is performed after 429, 5xx, timeout, `ECONNRESET`, malformed response, or ambiguous transport.
- The desired Track is reconstructed from the fresh normalized state in the original release order. Every active release and every supported field is preserved: name, versionCodes, status, userFraction, localized notes, country targeting, and in-app update priority. Only the target release's `userFraction` changes from the approved current value to the approved larger value. Fresh state changes, duplicate/missing target releases, unsupported/unroundtrippable data, malformed update responses, and pre-commit read-back mismatches block before commit; no raw Google object passthrough is used.
- Validation is fresh and precedes commit. Commit uses the existing Phase 4.10 Publisher boundary with explicit `changesInReviewBehavior=ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW`, explicit `changesNotSentForReview=false`, empty request body, and exactly one non-retried attempt. A confirmed response must match the operational edit identity; only then is the managed local session cleared and read back empty. Ambiguous commit retains the managed session/evidence, marks external state uncertain, and skips post-commit verification; explicit rejection may clean up the operational edit without retry.
- After confirmed commit, the workflow performs Phase 4.11 Layer A direct summary read first. If the release is not observed, it returns a bounded post-commit verification failure with no polling, no verification edit, and no second commit. If the local store is no longer empty, it returns `ROLLOUT_VERIFICATION_BLOCKED_BY_ACTIVE_EDIT` and does not create a verification edit.
- Exact post-commit verification reuses the Phase 4.11 temporary-edit mechanism under the original rollout approval: at most one verification `edits.insert`, exact target `edits.tracks.get`, comparison of versionCode set, release name, `inProgress` status, new fraction, release notes, country targeting, priority, unrelated releases, and release order, followed by exactly one non-retried `edits.delete`. The verification edit is never stored as the normal managed operational session. Cleanup failure is uncertain; no corrective rollout mutation is attempted.
- Safe success is `{targetTrack, versionCode, releaseName, previousFraction, newFraction, status:"inProgress", rolloutCommitted:true, liveRolloutVerified:true, servingPropagationVerified:false}`. No note text, edit id, approval token, digest, raw Track/AppEdit, credential, transport diagnostic, device-serving claim, CDN claim, or automatic polling is serialized. Safe audit records contain only allowlisted rollout identity/result/uncertainty metadata.
- Focused tests: **41/41 across 2 files** (`tests/releases-rollout.test.ts`, `tests/releases-rollout-integration.test.ts`). Coverage includes permission/empty input; no/denied/expired/mismatched approval; digest invalidation; summary privacy; 0.05→0.10, 0.10→0.25, 0.25→0.50; no-op/decrease/out-of-range/nonfinite; missing/ambiguous identity; inProgress/draft/halted/completed/unspecified; active-edit guard; one insert/update/validate/commit; retry false; state preservation; pre-commit read-back; validation and commit ambiguity; explicit review policy; session clear/read-back; direct post-commit absence; local session race; deep exact mismatch; one verification delete; cleanup uncertainty; no halt/resume/completion/decrease/upload/browser/network.

**Phase 4.13 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-30):**

- The installed `@googleapis/androidpublisher@42.1.0` declarations/generated client and current official Android Publisher documentation were inspected for `Schema$TrackRelease`, `TrackRelease.status`, `TrackRelease.userFraction`, `countryTargeting`, `inAppUpdatePriority`, `edits.insert`, `edits.get`, `edits.tracks.get`, `edits.tracks.update`, `edits.validate`, `edits.commit`, `edits.delete`, and direct `applications.tracks.releases.list`. Current Google semantics are represented without a separate halt/resume endpoint: HALT is `inProgress → halted`, RESUME is `halted → inProgress`; `userFraction` remains strictly between 0 and 1 and is unchanged.
- Two explicit operation-scoped capabilities are registered: `releases.halt_rollout` and `releases.resume_rollout`. Both are exactly `destructive`, require human approval, expose authoritative model input `{}`, and bind package, target track, versionCode-primary release identity, release name, expected current status, exact expected fraction, normalized Track digest, `ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW`, `changesNotSentForReview=false`, operation kind, and separate request digests. HALT and RESUME approvals cannot authorize each other and do not reuse Phase 4.9 commit, Phase 4.11 verification, or Phase 4.12 fraction approvals.
- HALT requires a fresh exact `inProgress` release; RESUME requires a fresh exact `halted` release. Missing/invalid fractions, no-op status, draft/completed/status-unspecified states, missing/ambiguous version identity, stale status, and stale fraction fail before `tracks.update`. A halted release never receives an invented fraction.
- The Phase 4.11 direct Layer A summary read is mandatory before operational `edits.insert`; absent/ambiguous direct observation returns with operational insert zero and no polling. The managed `ReleaseEditSessionStore` must be empty; an existing managed edit returns `MANAGED_EDIT_ALREADY_OPEN` and approval cannot override it.
- The operational lifecycle is exactly one no-retry `edits.insert`, exact id/expiry validation, normal managed-session persistence, same-edit `edits.get`/`edits.tracks.get`, one status-only `edits.tracks.update`, exact pre-commit Track read-back, `edits.validate`, and exactly one no-retry `edits.commit` with explicit `ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW` and `changesNotSentForReview=false`. Ambiguous insert/update/commit outcomes are never retried; ambiguous commit retains the managed evidence and skips post-commit verification.
- Fresh normalized Track state is reconstructed in the existing release order. Every active release preserves name, versionCodes, status, userFraction, localized notes, safely round-trippable country targeting, and in-app update priority. The target release changes only `status`; HALT preserves its fraction while setting `halted`, and RESUME preserves its fraction while setting `inProgress`. No rollout percentage, versionCode, notes, priority, unrelated release, ordering, or completion state is changed.
- Country-targeting policy is fail-closed: current official documentation permits setting `countryTargeting` only for `inProgress` releases in production and does not establish a lossless omission/recovery round-trip for a halted release. Therefore a desired Track containing country targeting with a non-`inProgress` release returns typed `ROLLOUT_STATE_NOT_ROUNDTRIPPABLE` before `tracks.update`; targeting is never silently cleared. RESUME can preserve a present target targeting field when the desired target status is `inProgress`. Existing in-app update priority is copied exactly; Phase 4.13 never intentionally changes it.
- After confirmed commit, the managed session is cleared and read back empty. Phase 4.11 Layer A runs first; only an observed release permits at most one temporary verification edit. Exact Layer B verification proves target status, exact unchanged fraction, version identity/name/set, notes, country targeting where safely supported, priority, unrelated releases, and ordering; the temporary edit is deleted exactly once with no retry. Cleanup failure is uncertain and never triggers another mutation. Results set `liveRolloutStateVerified:true` and `servingPropagationVerified:false`.
- Safe serializers and audit metadata omit edit ids, temporary edit ids, approval tokens, digests, notes, credentials, raw Google objects, and transport diagnostics. Only safe track/version/release/status/fraction/commit/verification metadata is returned. No upload, release creation, completion, fraction mutation, browser, live Google call, or automatic polling is in the Phase 4.13 boundary.
- Focused status-control tests: **16/16 across 2 files** (`tests/releases-status-control.test.ts`, `tests/releases-status-control-integration.test.ts`). Coverage includes separate destructive tools/approvals, empty authoritative input, exact status transitions, wrong/stale/missing state, fraction immutability, country-targeting fail-closed behavior, full-track preservation, managed-edit guard, one insert/update/validate/commit, retry-disabled policy, pre-commit read-back, session cleanup, direct/deep post-commit verification, safe cleanup uncertainty, and real Phase 2 runtime integration over a fake Publisher boundary.
- Full regression `NODE_ENV=development npm run test:run -- --reporter=dot` → **1362/1362 across 64 files**. Final local gates on Node v26.9.0: `NODE_ENV=development npm run build`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run format:check` all exit 0. - Runtime dependencies remain exactly 4; `package.json` hash is `d1ca499f3408e507e44d4ad7a88107f8da5ed786f8b58be38132f17718008a53`; `package-lock.json` hash is `8f8b2805eb7acdcea9dd67212071e5df84c24ab139e741d50b7348d58599017e`; SDK remains `42.1.0`; no Git commit was created. **Phase 4.14 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-09-30):**

- The exact roadmap wording is: `Dry-run support: every mutating release tool supports --dry-run printing the exact API call sequence it would make (edit create → upload → track update → validate → commit), without calling the API; unit-tested.` The repository inventory identified nine current mutating Release Agent capabilities: `releases.open_edit` (`destructive`), `releases.upload_bundle` (`write`), `releases.configure_release` (`write`), `releases.attach_release_notes` (`write`), `releases.commit_edit` (`publish`), `releases.verify_committed_release` (`destructive`), `releases.update_rollout_fraction` (`destructive`), `releases.halt_rollout` (`destructive`), and `releases.resume_rollout` (`destructive`). Read-only release tools were not wrapped.
- Added one PlayOps-owned normalized `ReleaseDryRunPlan` with version, tool name, real permission, real-run approval requirement, ordered `PlannedApiCall[]`, planned local actions, remote preconditions not checked during planning, `sideEffectsExecuted:false`, and zero execution evidence counters. All plans use one deterministic structured format and one renderer; human output is rendered from the structured plan and is never parsed back into behavior.
- “Exact API call sequence” is defined as exact operation name, HTTP method, static resource/path structure, static path/query parameters, request-body/media structure, retry policy, prior-result dependencies, and conditional execution. Values returned only by Google are represented symbolically through typed result references such as a later `edits.tracks.get` consuming the `editId` returned by an earlier `edits.insert`; no fake edit id, token, pagination cursor, or remote state is invented.
- Conditional operations are explicit. For Phase 4.11/4.12/4.13 verification, the direct release-summary read is followed by conditional temporary `edits.insert` → `edits.tracks.get` → `edits.delete` only when the expected release is observed and preconditions pass. The plan does not claim that conditional calls will definitely execute, and it does not omit them.
- The planner is pure and uses the same trusted/composition-bound operation intent types as real workflows. It has no Publisher/gateway, credential, token, network, browser, approval, audit, or session-store dependency; it never invokes a real tool, swaps a fake production gateway, rolls back, or consumes approval. Dry-run does not save/clear `ReleaseEditSessionStore`, append normal execution audit events, create ApprovalRequests, consume tokens, or mutate any local state.
- `releases.open_edit` plans one `POST edits.insert` with `retry:false` and no body. `releases.upload_bundle` plans managed `edits.get` → media `POST edits.bundles.upload` with `application/octet-stream`, `retry:false`, 120-second timeout semantics, and `GET edits.bundles.list`; the artifact path and bytes are omitted and `ackBundleInstallationWarning` remains absent. `releases.configure_release` plans fresh edit/bundle/track reads → one `PUT edits.tracks.update` → exact track read-back, with name/versionCodes/status/fraction shape and Phase 4.7 fields omitted. `releases.attach_release_notes` plans fresh reads → one full-track-preserving `tracks.update` where only `releaseNotes` differs → read-back; canonical languages and code-point lengths are visible while note text is redacted.
- `releases.commit_edit` plans fresh `edits.get`/`edits.tracks.get` → `POST edits.validate` → exactly one `POST edits.commit` with empty body, explicit `changesInReviewBehavior=ERROR_IF_IN_REVIEW`, `changesNotSentForReview=false`, and `retry:false`; local session clear/read-back is represented as a planned real-run action only. `releases.verify_committed_release` plans direct summary GET → conditional temporary insert/get/delete with destructive permission and approval required for a real run.
- `releases.update_rollout_fraction` plans the complete current workflow: direct summary → operational insert → edit/track reads → status-preserving full-track `tracks.update` with trusted current/requested fractions → pre-commit track read → validate → commit → direct post-commit summary → conditional deep verification. The plan states `newFraction > currentFraction`; no status change is planned. HALT and RESUME use distinct plans over the same lifecycle, with only target status changing (`inProgress → halted` or `halted → inProgress`) and exact `userFraction` preservation. Country-targeting round-trip constraints are represented as remote preconditions.
- Privacy policy is stricter than debug output: plans/rendered output omit credentials, Authorization/access tokens, approval capabilities/tokens, local session paths, raw edit IDs, raw Google payloads, AAB paths/bytes, and complete release-note text. Release-note shapes contain only language, `<operator-bound text redacted>`, and code-point count. Symbolic references are labels, not real IDs.
- The existing CLI had no release live-execution architecture; it contained `doctor` and `reviews` only. Phase 4.14 therefore adds the smallest operator adapter: `playops releases --dry-run <mutating-release-tool>` through `runReleasesCli`/`runCli`, with a trusted injected `ReleaseDryRunCliDeps.createPlan`. The model cannot supply `dryRun`; it is absent from every LLM tool schema. The default CLI does not initialize release credentials or a live release composition for this adapter; without a trusted composition-bound plan factory it fails safely before any API access. Normal non-dry-run release behavior and existing CLI behavior are unchanged.
- Focused tests: **13/13** in `tests/releases-dry-run.test.ts`. Coverage includes the complete nine-tool set, deterministic/immutable plans, symbolic values, conditional calls, permissions/approval metadata, upload media semantics, configure/notes request shapes, commit policy, deep verification, HALT/RESUME distinction, renderer stability, CLI parsing/routing, and real Phase 4.12 fake-gateway conformance against the planned operation sequence.
- Full regression `NODE_ENV=development npm run test:run -- --reporter=dot` → **1375/1375 across 65 files**. Final local gates on Node v26.9.0: `NODE_ENV=development npm run build`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run format:check` all exit 0. Built smoke `node /home/dhikrama/.hermes/cache/scratch/playops-phase414-built-smoke.mjs` → `BUILT_PHASE414_DRY_RUN_SMOKE_PASS { mutatingToolsCovered:9, apiCallsExecuted:0, networkCalls:0, browserCalls:0, approvalRequests:0, approvalTokensConsumed:0, sessionSaves:0, sessionClears:0, auditWrites:0, credentialsLoaded:0, artifactBytesUploaded:0 }`.
- Runtime dependencies remain exactly 4; package and lock hashes remain unchanged; Review Agent, Phase 2 semantics, and Phase 4.1–4.13 real execution remain full-suite verified; no live Google call, credential read, browser action, real AAB, dependency addition, or Git commit occurred. Phase 4.15 lifecycle hygiene was not started. B1 remains resolved only for previous connectivity/read evidence; B3 remains OPEN because Node 24 was not run.

**Phase 4.15 verified architecture and safety boundary (2026-10-02):**

- The exact roadmap wording is: `Edit lifecycle hygiene: abandoned/failed edits are detected and either deleted or reported; unit-tested.` Three PlayOps-owned pieces satisfy it: `src/releases/cleanup-journal.ts` (durable, versioned, atomic known-edit journal; allowlisted fields only), `src/releases/hygiene-tool.ts` (`releases.inspect_edit_hygiene`, permission `read`, no approval, no verifier, REPORT ONLY over the managed session plus every journalled verification edit), and `src/releases/cleanup-tool.ts` (`releases.cleanup_known_edit`, permission `destructive`, empty authoritative input, exact SHA-256 approval digest/summary bound to the exact known record, composition-bound candidate only — never model-selected, never a wildcard).
- **Live evidence accepted as evidence, not as a contract.** A controlled live probe on `com.dhikrama.driver` (2026-10-02, recorded in `docs/phase-4.15-b4-probe-plan.md` §16) produced the exact sequence `edits.insert` SUCCESS → returned id/expiry validated → cleanup-journal record persisted and read back → exact pre-delete `edits.get` SUCCESS → `edits.delete` SUCCESS in exactly one `retry:false` attempt → immediate post-delete `edits.get` FAILED with HTTP 400, `response.data.error.code` 400, `response.data.error.status` `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, and `errors[].reason` `failedPrecondition`. The retained journal still holds exactly that one record. The operator recorded explicitly that this single observation does **not** authorize a global `400 ⇒ inactive` or `FAILED_PRECONDITION ⇒ inactive` rule.
- **Safe structured boundary metadata.** `PublisherError` now carries only allowlisted machine-readable classification — `status` (numeric HTTP status through the pre-existing `safeStatus` chain), `googleStatus` (`response.data.error.status`), `googleReasons` (deduped, capped `response.data.error.errors[].reason`), and `transportCode` (a system code such as `ECONNRESET`, only when no HTTP response exists) — projected exactly once by the new pure, total `projectPublisherErrorMetadata`. Raw messages, headers, request config, URLs, credentials, tokens, response bodies, and the raw Gaxios object are never copied and never serialized; `code` semantics (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`/`API_REQUEST_FAILED`/`INVALID_RESPONSE`) and the human-safe message format are unchanged; `EDIT_DELETE_POLICY = { attempts: 1, retry: false }` documents the delete wrapper's guarantee. `ReleaseError` carries the same allowlisted classification (`publisherCode`/`status`/`googleStatus`/`googleReasons`/`transportCode`), populated only by the release gateway adapter, so no Google SDK/Gaxios type reaches `src/releases` or the runtime.
- **The classifier is CONTEXTUAL, never global.** New pure module `src/releases/post-delete-verification.ts` exposes `classifyPostDeleteEditRead`. It returns `REMOTE_INACTIVE` ONLY when every trusted workflow-context condition holds simultaneously: the same package and the same exact edit identity on all three legs (validated through the existing production `parseGooglePlayEditSession`), the pre-delete read succeeded, the delete was acknowledged with `attempts === 1` and retry disabled, the post-delete read failed, and the failure carries `code === "API_REQUEST_FAILED"`, `status === 400`, `googleStatus === "FAILED_PRECONDITION"`, and `googleReasons` containing `failedPrecondition`. Any missing, mismatched, or unexpected element yields `UNKNOWN` with a closed reason token; `400` alone, `FAILED_PRECONDITION` alone, `failedPrecondition` alone, `API_REQUEST_FAILED` alone, and every 401/403/429/5xx/timeout/ECONNRESET/unclassified outcome are all `UNKNOWN`. The module is total (hostile getters ⇒ `UNKNOWN`) and holds no Google type, no raw response navigation, and no I/O.
- **`releases.cleanup_known_edit` completes the ACTIVE path.** Execution validates the exact bound local record, short-circuits a trusted local expiry with zero Google calls, otherwise performs one fresh exact `edits.get`, then EXACTLY ONE `edits.delete` (a single linear call — no loop and no PlayOps read retry — over a wrapper whose generated retry is disabled), then one post-delete `edits.get` of that same exact identity, then the narrow contextual verdict. Local reconciliation and read-back happen only for the verified verdict. A post-delete read that unexpectedly succeeds, any unverified tuple, an unacknowledged delete, and every pre-delete failure all retain the record, issue no second delete, clear nothing local, and report external state uncertainty where a remote mutation may have occurred.
- **Hygiene stays conservative on purpose.** `releases.inspect_edit_hygiene` is unchanged: a bare `edits.get` failure — including the exact observed tuple — is still reported `unknown`/`remote_state_undecidable`, because standalone inspection has no confirmed-delete context. The live observation was therefore never turned into an undocumented global Google contract.
- **Dry-run matches the real sequence.** The Phase 4.14 plan for `releases.cleanup_known_edit` describes the fresh exact state check → conditional single retry-disabled `edits.delete` → post-delete exact `edits.get` → contextual verification → conditional local reconciliation, with zero API/approval/session/journal/audit effects, and it marks the delete's condition as `exactly ONE attempt with retries disabled` instead of the previous not-executed placeholder.
- **Tests.** Focused `tests/releases-post-delete-verification.test.ts` (complete context-binding matrix, the observed tuple with and without confirmed-delete context, every transient/auth case, malformed/hostile input, determinism, isolation scan), `tests/publisher-error-metadata.test.ts` (allowlisted projection per status, transport-code rule, dedupe/cap, totality, no leak through `JSON.stringify`, exactly one delete attempt, unchanged bounded read retry), the rewritten `tests/releases-edit-cleanup.test.ts`, and `tests/releases-edit-cleanup-integration.test.ts`, which drives the whole compiled chain (fake generated client → production publisher boundary → release gateway adapter → cleanup tool → classifier) through the REAL `ToolRegistry`, `runAgent`, permission engine, approval gate, and verification machinery with temp session/journal/audit. Coverage includes exact ordering `edits.get:pre → edits.delete → edits.get:post`, exactly one delete, record removal and read-back for the verified verdict, and fail-closed retention with `externalStateUncertain: true` for `ok`/404/403/429/500/ECONNRESET.
- Full regression `NODE_ENV=development npm run test:run` → **1453/1453 across 69 files** (1407/66 before this work). All five gates exit 0 on Node v26.9.0: `NODE_ENV=development npm run build`, `npm run test:run`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run format:check`. Built fake-only smoke `node /home/dhikrama/.hermes/cache/scratch/playops-phase415-built-smoke.mjs` → `BUILT_PHASE415_EDIT_HYGIENE_SMOKE PASS { checks:77, failed:0, mutatingToolsCovered:10, networkCalls:0, browserCalls:0, getEditCalls:31, remoteDeleteCalls:8, insertCalls:0, commitCalls:0, trackUpdateCalls:0, phase2Verifications:2 }` — the 8 remote deletes are 1 verified path plus 7 correctly refused unverified outcomes.
- Runtime dependencies remain exactly 4; `package.json` and `package-lock.json` hashes are unchanged; the Review Agent and Phase 2/3 semantics were not modified. Acceptance made **no live Google call**, no credential read, no browser action, no Google `edits.insert`/`edits.delete`, and no Git commit. The retained B4 probe journal `/tmp/playops-b4-probe-TX5SKW/edit-cleanup-journal.json` was left untouched (sha256 `64c263e8…`) and deliberately not reconciled: it carries no confirmed-delete context inside the production workflow, so ordinary hygiene inspection still reports it UNKNOWN — the intended conservative behaviour, which does not block acceptance.
- Honest limitations retained: the single controlled observation on `com.dhikrama.driver` is evidence for that workflow context, not a permanent Google contract, and is unproven for superseded/expired edits, other applications, or Google's future behaviour; `FAILED_PRECONDITION` stays generic outside the confirmed-delete context; the irreducible `edits.insert`-response window (no `edits.list`, so an unpersisted remote id is unrecoverable) remains; journal atomicity remains single-process only; and there is still no operator CLI surface for candidate selection.
